NOW I HAVE THE FULL PICTURE. Let me summarize my findings:

## Summary of Findings

The desloppify queue logic shows that **subjective_assessment items should only appear when the queue is "cleared" (at start or end of the workflow).** Currently, they're appearing in the work queue when they shouldn't be.

Here's what's happening:

### How the Queue is Built (`build_work_queue` in `core.py`):

1. **Gathers items** from three sources:
   - Issue items from `build_issue_items()` 
   - **Subjective dimension items** from `build_subjective_items()` (line 113)
   - Workflow items from `build_triage_stage_items()`, score checkpoints, plan creation, etc.

2. **Key flow**: Line 113 in `core.py`:
   ```python
   items += _gather_subjective_items(state, opts, threshold)
   ```

3. **Subjective item filtering** (lines 179-199 in `synthetic.py`):
   - Iterates through all subjective entries from `all_subjective_entries()`
   - Only creates queue items if `strict_val < threshold` (line 213)
   - With threshold=100.0, ALL dimensions with strict score < 100 get turned into subjective_dimension items

### The Problem: Subjective Items Are Always Added

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py` (line 193), the queue is built with:
```python
queue = build_work_queue(
    state,
    options=QueueBuildOptions(
        count=None,
        status="open",
        include_subjective=True,  # <-- Always True!
        plan=plan,
        include_skipped=include_skipped,
        cluster=effective_cluster,
    ),
)
```

The `include_subjective=True` means that:
- **All dimensions with score < 100** get turned into `subjective_dimension` items
- These items are added to the queue unconditionally
- They appear in the queue display regardless of whether objective work remains

### Why This Is a Bug:

According to the queue display logic (lines 42-56 in `queue_render.py`):
- `subjective` = count of items where `kind == "subjective_dimension"`
- When you run `desloppify plan queue`, it shows all these synthetic items

The message "Queue: 20 items (16 planned · 55 skipped · 20 subjective)" means:
- 20 total items in queue
- 16 are from `queue_order` (the planned mechanical work)  
- 55 are skipped
- 20 are synthetic subjective_dimension items

**The bug**: There should be a lifecycle gate that prevents subjective items from appearing in the queue when there's still objective work to do. Currently, they're always included.

### Key Code Locations:

1. **Queue building**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py` lines 90-145 (`build_work_queue`)
2. **Subjective item creation**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/synthetic.py` lines 166-259 (`build_subjective_items`)
3. **Lifecycle filtering**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py` lines 124, 227-235 (`_apply_lifecycle_filter`)
4. **Queue display**: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py` lines 29-76 (`_print_queue_header` and `_gather_subjective_items`)

The `_apply_lifecycle_filter` on line 124 is supposed to handle this, but let me verify it's working correctly.

   - Added `priority: int` field to Cluster
   - Bumped PLAN_VERSION from 7 to 8
   - Updated import from `upgrade_plan_to_v7` to `upgrade_plan_to_v8`

3. **Phase 2 - Step Parser**: Created new file `step_parser.py` with `parse_steps_file()`, `format_steps()`, `normalize_step()`, `step_summary()`

4. **Phase 3 - Schema Migration**: Added `upgrade_plan_to_v8()` function that chains through v7, then converts flat string action_steps to ActionStep dicts with a heuristic (≤120 chars = title only, >120 = first sentence = title, rest = detail)

5. **Phase 4 - CLI Args**: Added --steps-file, --add-step, --detail, --update-step, --remove-step, --done-step, --undone-step, --priority to cluster update. Added --priority and --steps-file to cluster create. Added new `export` and `import` subcommands.

6. **Phase 5 - Handler Updates**: 
   - Rewrote `_cmd_cluster_update` to handle all new args
   - Added `_print_step()` helper for structured step display
   - Updated `_cmd_cluster_show` with priority display and structured steps
   - Updated `_cmd_cluster_list` (verbose) with Priority column
   - Updated `_cmd_cluster_list` (non-verbose) with [P1] inline tags
   - Updated `_sorted_clusters_by_queue_pos` to sort by (priority, min_queue_pos)
   - Added `_cmd_cluster_export` (text + YAML)
   - Added `_cmd_cluster_import` (YAML bulk)
   - Updated dispatch table

7. **Test Fixes**: Fixed several tests:
   - `test_plan_version_is_7` → `test_plan_version_is_current` (uses PLAN_VERSION)
   - `test_cluster_merge.py` - Updated to use ActionStep dicts instead of flat strings
   - `test_cluster_ux.py` - Updated `_fake_args` defaults, replaced obsolete zero-steps/long-step warning tests with `test_cluster_update_with_add_step`

8. **Queue Investigation**: User noticed the queue was in a weird state. I investigated:
   - Found the queue showing only subjective_assessment items
   - Discovered `queue_order` had 4 triage IDs (observe, reflect, organize, commit) with only 3 confirmed
   - Found the real plan.json was in the reigh project (.desloppify/plan.json), not the desloppify project
   - Discovered 16 real issue IDs + 18 subjective:: entries in queue_order
   - All 16 real issues had impact score 0.0467, just under the 0.05 `_MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT` floor
   - The impact floor was filtering ALL planned items because Code quality dimension was at 99.3/100

9. **Impact Floor Fix**: First made plan-ordered items bypass the floor, then user asked to remove it entirely. Removed `_MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT`, `_passes_impact_floor()`, and the filter line. Updated tests.

10. **Queue Review**: User asked me to review the full queue for "code monkey readiness". I found:
    - All manual clusters have 0 members despite having detailed steps
    - No priority field actually set (just text in descriptions)
    - Auto-clusters have no steps
    - Some clusters need judgment despite claiming otherwise

11. **Empty Cluster Investigation**: User asked me to test adding items to clusters. Adding worked fine. Investigation revealed:
    - All review issues were `auto_resolved` with note "Fixed despite wontfix — disappeared from scan (was wontfix)"
    - They had been `wontfix` status, then a scan auto-resolved them
    - `find_suspect_detectors` has a bug: it only adds `review` to suspect set for detectors with OPEN issues, but these were `wontfix`, so the protection never triggered

12. **Root Cause Found**: The bug is in `find_suspect_detectors()` in `merge_issues.py`:
    - Lines 19-26 only collect detectors that have `status == "open"` issues
    - Lines 33-36 only check `import_only_detectors` for detectors in that set
    - If all review issues are `wontfix` (not `open`), `review` never appears in `previous_open_by_detector`, so the `import_only_detectors` check never fires
    - The wontfix review issues then fall through to auto-resolution at lines 122-133

The conversation ended with me having identified the exact bug but not yet fixing it. The user's last question was "Your task is to create a detailed summary" which is this summary request.

The pending fix is straightforward: `import_only_detectors` should be added to the suspect set unconditionally, not dependent on having open issues.
</analysis>

<summary>
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - **Primary**: Implement a comprehensive plan to improve the cluster steps system in desloppify, including structured ActionStep data model, step file parser, schema migration, CLI args, bulk import/export, display improvements, and priority-based sorting.
   - **Secondary**: User noticed the queue was in a broken state after implementation and asked me to investigate why. This led to discovering and fixing the impact floor bug, then investigating why all manual clusters had 0 members, ultimately revealing a bug in `find_suspect_detectors()` that allows review/wontfix issues to be auto-resolved by scans.
   - **Tertiary**: User asked me to review the full queue for "code monkey readiness" — whether an agent could execute the entire queue without mistakes based on available instructions.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **ActionStep TypedDict**: New structured step format with title, detail, issue_refs, done fields
   - **Schema migration v7→v8**: Converts flat string action_steps to ActionStep dicts with heuristic parsing
   - **Step parser**: Numbered-steps text format that round-trips with parse/format functions
   - **Impact floor removal**: `_MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT = 0.05` was a pre-planning vestigial filter that silently dropped items when dimension scores got high (headroom → 0)
   - **Queue pipeline**: Gather → Score → Presort (plan-aware) → Lifecycle filter → Sort → Limit
   - **Suspect detectors bug**: `find_suspect_detectors()` only protects `import_only_detectors` (like "review") when they have open issues, missing wontfix issues entirely
   - **Reconciliation**: `_supersede_id()` removes issue IDs from ALL cluster `issue_ids`, which gutted triage-built clusters when review findings were auto-resolved
   - **Plan-native vs state-derived work items**: Fundamental design gap — the system has no concept of durable work items that aren't scanner findings

     ```python
     def upgrade_plan_to_v8(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
         changed = upgrade_plan_to_v7(plan)
         for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
             if isinstance(cluster, dict):
                 if _migrate_action_steps_to_v8(cluster):
                     changed = True
         if plan.get("version") != V8_SCHEMA_VERSION:
             plan["version"] = V8_SCHEMA_VERSION
             changed = True
         return changed
     ```

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Cluster steps system**: Fully implemented (schema, parser, migration, CLI, handlers, display, export/import).
   - **Empty queue bug**: Solved by removing the impact floor — a pre-planning vestigial filter that silently dropped items when scores got high.
   - **Empty cluster members bug**: Root cause fully traced. `find_suspect_detectors()` only protects `import_only_detectors` when they have open issues. When all review issues were wontfix, they were auto-resolved by the next scan, then reconciliation removed them from all clusters. Fix identified but not yet applied.

7. Pending Tasks:
   - **Fix `find_suspect_detectors()` bug**: The `import_only_detectors` set must be added to `suspect` unconditionally, not just when detectors appear in `previous_open_by_detector`. This is the direct cause of all manual clusters having 0 members.
   - **Set priority fields on manual clusters**: The clusters have "PRIORITY 1", "PRIORITY 2" etc. in their description text but the actual `priority` field (which we just built) is unset on all of them.

8. Current Work:
   I had just traced the root cause of why all 64 review issues were auto-resolved despite the `import_only_detectors` protection in `find_suspect_detectors()`. The bug is at lines 33-36 of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/merge_issues.py`:
   
   ```python
   for detector, previous_count in previous_open_by_detector.items():
       if detector in import_only_detectors:
           suspect.add(detector)
           continue
   ```
   
   This loop only iterates detectors that have **open** issues (built at lines 19-26). When all review issues are `wontfix`, the `review` detector never appears in `previous_open_by_detector`, so the `import_only_detectors` check never fires. The wontfix review issues then fall through to auto-resolution at lines 122-133.
   
   I confirmed this with a test showing `find_suspect_detectors(issues, {}, force_resolve=False)` returns `review` NOT in the suspect set.
   
   The user's last substantive question was "Why is this possible in the first place?" and I had just shown them the exact code that fails.

9. Optional Next Step:
   Fix the `find_suspect_detectors()` bug in `merge_issues.py`. The fix is to add `import_only_detectors` to the suspect set unconditionally, before the loop over `previous_open_by_detector`. This directly continues the investigation the user asked for with "Why is this possible in the first place?" — we found the bug, the natural next step is to fix it. The fix should also check all statuses (not just open) for import-only detectors so wontfix review items are protected.

   154→    version: Required[int]

   162→    clusters: dict[str, Cluster]
   163→    superseded: dict[str, SupersededEntry]
   164→    promoted_ids: list[str]  # IDs user explicitly positioned via move_items()
   165→    plan_start_scores: PlanStartScores
   166→    execution_log: list[ExecutionLogEntry]
   167→    epic_triage_meta: EpicTriageMeta
   168→    commit_log: list[CommitRecord]
   169→    uncommitted_issues: list[str]
   170→    commit_tracking_branch: str | None
   171→    completed_clusters: NotRequired[list[dict[str, Any]]]  # legacy snapshot key
   172→
   173→
   174→def empty_plan() -> PlanModel:
   175→    """Return a new empty plan payload."""
   176→    now = utc_now()
   177→    return {
   178→        "version": PLAN_VERSION,
   179→        "created": now,
   180→        "updated": now,
   181→        "queue_order": [],
   182→        "deferred": [],
   183→        "skipped": {},
   184→        "active_cluster": None,
   185→        "overrides": {},
   186→        "clusters": {},
   187→        "superseded": {},
   188→        "promoted_ids": [],
   189→        "plan_start_scores": {},
   190→        "execution_log": [],
   191→        "epic_triage_meta": {},
   192→        "commit_log": [],
   193→        "uncommitted_issues": [],
   194→        "commit_tracking_branch": None,
   195→    }
   196→
   197→
   198→def ensure_plan_defaults(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   199→    """Normalize a loaded plan to ensure all keys exist.
   200→
   201→    Runtime contract is v8-only. Legacy payloads are upgraded in-place once.
   202→    """
   203→    defaults = empty_plan()
   204→    for key, value in defaults.items():
   205→        plan.setdefault(key, value)
   206→    _upgrade_plan_to_v8(plan)
   207→
   208→
   209→def triage_clusters(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Cluster]:
   210→    """Return clusters whose name starts with ``EPIC_PREFIX``."""
   211→    return {
   212→        name: cluster
   213→        for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items()
   214→        if name.startswith(EPIC_PREFIX)
   215→    }
   216→
   217→
   218→def validate_plan(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   219→    """Raise ValueError when plan invariants are violated."""
   220→    if not isinstance(plan.get("version"), int):
   221→        raise ValueError("plan.version must be an int")
   222→    if not isinstance(plan.get("queue_order"), list):
   223→        raise ValueError("plan.queue_order must be a list")
   224→
   225→    # No ID should appear in both queue_order and skipped
   226→    skipped_ids = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   227→    overlap = set(plan["queue_order"]) & skipped_ids
   228→    if overlap:
   229→        raise ValueError(
   230→            f"IDs cannot appear in both queue_order and skipped: {sorted(overlap)}"
   231→        )
   232→
   233→    # Validate skip entry kinds
   234→    for fid, entry in plan.get("skipped", {}).items():
   235→        kind = entry.get("kind")
   236→        if kind not in VALID_SKIP_KINDS:
   237→            raise ValueError(
   238→                f"Invalid skip kind {kind!r} for {fid}; must be one of {sorted(VALID_SKIP_KINDS)}"
   239→            )
   240→
   241→

1→"""Post-scan plan reconciliation — handle issue churn."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     6→from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.annotations import get_issue_note
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.operations_meta import append_log_entry
    10→from desloppify.engine._plan.operations_skip import resurface_stale_skips
    11→from desloppify.engine._plan.promoted_ids import prune_promoted_ids
    12→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
    13→    EPIC_PREFIX,
    14→    PlanModel,
    15→    SupersededEntry,
    16→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    17→)
    18→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import (
    19→    SYNTHETIC_PREFIXES,
    20→    compute_new_issue_ids,
    21→    sync_triage_needed,
    22→)
    23→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, utc_now
    24→
    25→SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS = 90
    26→
    27→
    28→@dataclass
    29→class ReconcileResult:
    30→    """Summary of changes made during reconciliation."""
    31→
    32→    superseded: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    33→    pruned: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    34→    resurfaced: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    35→    changes: int = 0
    36→
    37→
    38→def _find_candidates(
    39→    state: StateModel, detector: str, file: str
    40→) -> list[str]:
    41→    """Find open issues that could be remaps for a disappeared issue."""
    42→    candidates: list[str] = []
    43→    for fid, issue in state.get("issues", {}).items():
    44→        if issue.get("status") != "open":
    45→            continue
    46→        if issue.get("detector") == detector and issue.get("file") == file:
    47→            candidates.append(fid)
    48→    return candidates
    49→
    50→
    51→def _is_issue_alive(state: StateModel, issue_id: str) -> bool:
    52→    """Return True if the issue exists and is open."""
    53→    issue = state.get("issues", {}).get(issue_id)
    54→    if issue is None:
    55→        return False
    56→    return issue.get("status") == "open"
    57→
    58→
    59→def _supersede_id(
    60→    plan: PlanModel,
    61→    state: StateModel,
    62→    issue_id: str,
    63→    now: str,
    64→) -> bool:
    65→    """Move a disappeared issue to superseded. Returns True if changed."""
    66→    issue = state.get("issues", {}).get(issue_id)
    67→    detector = ""
    68→    file = ""
    69→    summary = ""
    70→    if issue:
    71→        detector = issue.get("detector", "")
    72→        file = issue.get("file", "")
    73→        summary = issue.get("summary", "")
    74→
    75→    candidates = _find_candidates(state, detector, file) if detector else []
    76→    # Don't include the original in candidates
    77→    candidates = [c for c in candidates if c != issue_id]
    78→
    79→    entry: SupersededEntry = {
    80→        "original_id": issue_id,
    81→        "original_detector": detector,
    82→        "original_file": file,
    83→        "original_summary": summary,
    84→        "status": "superseded",
    85→        "superseded_at": now,
    86→        "remapped_to": None,
    87→        "candidates": candidates[:5],
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    # Preserve any existing override note
    91→    override_note = get_issue_note(plan, issue_id)
    92→    if override_note:
    93→        entry["note"] = override_note
    94→
    95→    plan["superseded"][issue_id] = entry
    96→
    97→    # Remove from queue_order, skipped, promoted_ids, cluster issue_ids
    98→    order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    99→    skipped: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
   100→    if issue_id in order:
   101→        order.remove(issue_id)
   102→    skipped.pop(issue_id, None)
   103→    prune_promoted_ids(plan, {issue_id})
   104→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   105→        ids = cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
   106→        if issue_id in ids:
   107→            ids.remove(issue_id)
   108→
   109→    # Clear stale cluster reference from override
   110→    override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(issue_id)
   111→    if override and override.get("cluster"):
   112→        override["cluster"] = None
   113→        override["updated_at"] = now
   114→
   115→    return True
   116→
   117→
   118→def _prune_old_superseded(plan: PlanModel, now_dt: datetime) -> list[str]:
   119→    """Remove superseded entries older than TTL. Returns pruned IDs."""
   120→    superseded = plan.get("superseded", {})
   121→    cutoff = now_dt - timedelta(days=SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS)
   122→    to_prune: list[str] = []
   123→
   124→    for fid, entry in superseded.items():
   125→        ts = entry.get("superseded_at", "")
   126→        try:
   127→            entry_dt = datetime.fromisoformat(ts)
   128→            if entry_dt.tzinfo is None:
   129→                entry_dt = entry_dt.replace(tzinfo=UTC)
   130→            if entry_dt < cutoff:
   131→                to_prune.append(fid)
   132→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   133→            to_prune.append(fid)
   134→
   135→    for fid in to_prune:
   136→        superseded.pop(fid, None)
   137→        # Also clean up stale overrides
   138→        plan.get("overrides", {}).pop(fid, None)
   139→
   140→    return to_prune
   141→
   142→
   143→def reconcile_plan_after_scan(
   144→    plan: PlanModel,
   145→    state: StateModel,
   146→) -> ReconcileResult:
   147→    """Reconcile plan against current state after a scan.
   148→
   149→    Finds IDs referenced in the plan that no longer exist or are no longer
   150→    open, moves them to superseded, and prunes old superseded entries.
   151→    """
   152→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   153→    result = ReconcileResult()
   154→    now = utc_now()
   155→    now_dt = datetime.now(UTC)
   156→
   157→    # Collect all issue IDs referenced by the plan
   158→    referenced_ids: set[str] = set()
   159→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   160→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   161→    for override_id in plan.get("overrides", {}):
   162→        referenced_ids.add(override_id)
   163→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   164→        referenced_ids.update(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
   165→
   166→    # Exclude already-superseded IDs and synthetic IDs (managed by stale_dimensions)
   167→    already_superseded = set(plan.get("superseded", {}).keys())
   168→    referenced_ids -= already_superseded
   169→    referenced_ids = {
   170→        fid for fid in referenced_ids
   171→        if not any(fid.startswith(prefix) for prefix in SYNTHETIC_PREFIXES)
   172→    }
   173→
   174→    # Check each referenced ID
   175→    for fid in sorted(referenced_ids):
   176→        if not _is_issue_alive(state, fid):
   177→            if _supersede_id(plan, state, fid, now):
   178→                result.superseded.append(fid)
   179→                result.changes += 1
   180→
   181→    # Reconcile epic clusters: remove dead issues, delete empty epics
   182→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   183→    epic_names_to_delete: list[str] = []
   184→    for name, cluster in list(clusters.items()):
   185→        if not name.startswith(EPIC_PREFIX):
   186→            continue
   187→        issue_ids = cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
   188→        alive_ids = [fid for fid in issue_ids if _is_issue_alive(state, fid)]
   189→        if alive_ids != issue_ids:
   190→            cluster["issue_ids"] = alive_ids
   191→            result.changes += 1
   192→        if not alive_ids:
   193→            epic_names_to_delete.append(name)
   194→    for name in epic_names_to_delete:
   195→        clusters.pop(name, None)
   196→        result.changes += 1
   197→
   198→    # Resurface stale temporary skips
   199→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   200→
   201→    resurfaced = resurface_stale_skips(plan, scan_count)
   202→    if resurfaced:
   203→        result.resurfaced = resurfaced
   204→        result.changes += len(resurfaced)
   205→
   206→    # Prune old superseded entries
   207→    pruned = _prune_old_superseded(plan, now_dt)
   208→    result.pruned = pruned
   209→    result.changes += len(pruned)
   210→
   211→    # Log reconciliation if any changes were made
   212→    if result.changes > 0:
   213→        append_log_entry(
   214→            plan,
   215→            "reconcile",
   216→            issue_ids=result.superseded,
   217→            actor="system",
   218→            detail={
   219→                "superseded_count": len(result.superseded),
   220→                "pruned_count": len(result.pruned),
   221→                "resurfaced_count": len(result.resurfaced),
   222→            },
   223→        )
   224→
   225→    return result
   226→
   227→
   228→@dataclass
   229→class ReviewImportSyncResult:
   230→    """Summary of plan changes after a review import."""
   231→
   232→    new_ids: set[str]
   233→    added_to_queue: list[str]
   234→    triage_injected: bool
   235→
   236→
   237→def sync_plan_after_review_import(
   238→    plan: PlanModel,
   239→    state: StateModel,
   240→) -> ReviewImportSyncResult | None:
   241→    """Sync plan queue after review import. Pure engine function — no I/O.
   242→
   243→    Appends new issue IDs to queue_order and injects triage stages
   244→    if needed. Returns None when there are no new issues to sync.
   245→    """
   246→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   247→    new_ids = compute_new_issue_ids(plan, state)
   248→    if not new_ids:
   249→        return None
   250→
   251→    # Add new issue IDs to end of queue_order so they have position
   252→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   253→    existing = set(order)
   254→    added: list[str] = []
   255→    for fid in sorted(new_ids):
   256→        if fid not in existing:
   257→            order.append(fid)
   258→            added.append(fid)
   259→
   260→    # Inject triage stages if needed
   261→    triage_result = sync_triage_needed(plan, state)
   262→    triage_injected = bool(
   263→        triage_result and getattr(triage_result, "injected", False)
   264→    )
   265→
   266→    return ReviewImportSyncResult(
   267→        new_ids=new_ids,
   268→        added_to_queue=added,
   269→        triage_injected=triage_injected,
   270→    )
   271→
   272→
   273→__all__ = [
   274→    "ReconcileResult",
   275→    "ReviewImportSyncResult",
   276→    "reconcile_plan_after_scan",
   277→    "sync_plan_after_review_import",
   278→]
   279→

1→"""Cluster mutations for plan operations."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.operations_queue import move_items
     6→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import Cluster, PlanModel, ensure_plan_defaults
     7→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
     8→
     9→
    10→def create_cluster(
    11→    plan: PlanModel,
    12→    name: str,
    13→    description: str | None = None,
    14→    action: str | None = None,
    15→) -> Cluster:
    16→    """Create a named cluster. Raises ValueError if it already exists."""
    17→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    18→    if name.startswith("auto/"):
    19→        raise ValueError(
    20→            f"Cluster names starting with 'auto/' are reserved for auto-clusters: {name!r}"
    21→        )
    22→    if name.startswith("epic/"):
    23→        raise ValueError(
    24→            f"Cluster names starting with 'epic/' are reserved for triage epics: {name!r}"
    25→        )
    26→    if name in plan["clusters"]:
    27→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} already exists")
    28→    now = utc_now()
    29→    cluster: Cluster = {
    30→        "name": name,
    31→        "description": description,
    32→        "issue_ids": [],
    33→        "created_at": now,
    34→        "updated_at": now,
    35→        "auto": False,
    36→        "cluster_key": "",
    37→        "action": action,
    38→        "user_modified": False,
    39→    }
    40→    plan["clusters"][name] = cluster
    41→    return cluster
    42→
    43→
    44→def add_to_cluster(
    45→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, issue_ids: list[str]
    46→) -> int:
    47→    """Add issue IDs to a cluster. Returns count added."""
    48→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    49→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
    50→    if cluster is None:
    51→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
    52→
    53→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["issue_ids"]
    54→    count = 0
    55→    now = utc_now()
    56→    for fid in issue_ids:
    57→        if fid not in member_ids:
    58→            member_ids.append(fid)
    59→            count += 1
    60→        overrides = plan["overrides"]
    61→        if fid not in overrides:
    62→            overrides[fid] = {"issue_id": fid, "created_at": now}
    63→        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = cluster_name
    64→        overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
    65→
    66→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
    67→    return count
    68→
    69→
    70→def remove_from_cluster(
    71→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, issue_ids: list[str]
    72→) -> int:
    73→    """Remove issue IDs from a cluster. Returns count removed."""
    74→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    75→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
    76→    if cluster is None:
    77→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
    78→
    79→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["issue_ids"]
    80→    now = utc_now()
    81→    count = 0
    82→    for fid in issue_ids:
    83→        if fid in member_ids:
    84→            member_ids.remove(fid)
    85→            count += 1
    86→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
    87→        if override and override.get("cluster") == cluster_name:
    88→            override["cluster"] = None
    89→            override["updated_at"] = now
    90→
    91→    if count > 0 and cluster.get("auto"):
    92→        cluster["user_modified"] = True
    93→
    94→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
    95→    return count
    96→
    97→
    98→def delete_cluster(plan: PlanModel, name: str) -> list[str]:
    99→    """Delete a cluster and clear cluster refs from overrides. Returns orphaned IDs."""
   100→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   101→    cluster = plan["clusters"].pop(name, None)
   102→    if cluster is None:
   103→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} does not exist")
   104→
   105→    orphaned = list(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
   106→    now = utc_now()
   107→    for fid in orphaned:
   108→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   109→        if override and override.get("cluster") == name:
   110→            override["cluster"] = None
   111→            override["updated_at"] = now
   112→
   113→    if plan.get("active_cluster") == name:
   114→        plan["active_cluster"] = None
   115→
   116→    return orphaned
   117→
   118→
   119→def merge_clusters(
   120→    plan: PlanModel, source_name: str, target_name: str
   121→) -> tuple[int, list[str]]:
   122→    """Move all source issues to target, copy missing metadata, delete source."""
   123→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   124→    if source_name == target_name:
   125→        raise ValueError("Cannot merge a cluster into itself")
   126→    source = plan["clusters"].get(source_name)
   127→    if source is None:
   128→        raise ValueError(f"Source cluster {source_name!r} does not exist")
   129→    target = plan["clusters"].get(target_name)
   130→    if target is None:
   131→        raise ValueError(f"Target cluster {target_name!r} does not exist")
   132→
   133→    source_ids = list(source.get("issue_ids", []))
   134→    target_ids: list[str] = target["issue_ids"]
   135→    now = utc_now()
   136→
   137→    existing = set(target_ids)
   138→    added = 0
   139→    for fid in source_ids:
   140→        if fid not in existing:
   141→            target_ids.append(fid)
   142→            existing.add(fid)
   143→            added += 1
   144→        overrides = plan["overrides"]
   145→        if fid not in overrides:
   146→            overrides[fid] = {"issue_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   147→        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = target_name
   148→        overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   149→
   150→    if not target.get("description") and source.get("description"):
   151→        target["description"] = source["description"]
   152→    if not target.get("action_steps") and source.get("action_steps"):
   153→        target["action_steps"] = list(source["action_steps"])
   154→    if not target.get("action") and source.get("action"):
   155→        target["action"] = source["action"]
   156→
   157→    target["updated_at"] = now
   158→
   159→    plan["clusters"].pop(source_name, None)
   160→    if plan.get("active_cluster") == source_name:
   161→        plan["active_cluster"] = None
   162→
   163→    return added, source_ids
   164→
   165→
   166→def move_cluster(
   167→    plan: PlanModel,
   168→    cluster_name: str,
   169→    position: str,
   170→    target: str | None = None,
   171→    offset: int | None = None,
   172→) -> int:
   173→    """Move all cluster members as a contiguous block. Returns count moved."""
   174→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   175→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   176→    if cluster is None:
   177→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   178→
   179→    member_ids = list(cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
   180→    if not member_ids:
   181→        return 0
   182→
   183→    return move_items(plan, member_ids, position, target, offset)
   184→
   185→
   186→__all__ = [
   187→    "add_to_cluster",
   188→    "create_cluster",
   189→    "delete_cluster",
   190→    "merge_clusters",
   191→    "move_cluster",
   192→    "remove_from_cluster",
   193→]

1→"""Plan-order and cluster-collapse helpers for work queues."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Any
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.registry import DETECTORS
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.annotations import (
     9→    get_issue_description,
    10→    get_issue_note,
    11→    get_issue_override,
    12→)
    13→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.types import WorkQueueItem
    14→from desloppify.state import StateModel
    15→
    16→
    17→def new_item_ids(state: StateModel) -> set[str]:
    18→    """Return issue IDs added in the most recent scan."""
    19→    scan_history = state.get("scan_history", [])
    20→    if not scan_history:
    21→        return set()
    22→    threshold = scan_history[-1].get("timestamp", "")
    23→    if not threshold:
    24→        return set()
    25→    return {
    26→        issue_id
    27→        for issue_id, issue in state.get("issues", {}).items()
    28→        if issue.get("first_seen", "") >= threshold
    29→    }
    30→
    31→
    32→def enrich_plan_metadata(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> None:
    33→    """Stamp plan description, note, and cluster info from overrides."""
    34→    clusters: dict = plan.get("clusters", {})
    35→
    36→    for item in items:
    37→        issue_id = item["id"]
    38→        description = get_issue_description(plan, issue_id)
    39→        note = get_issue_note(plan, issue_id)
    40→        override = get_issue_override(plan, issue_id)
    41→        if description:
    42→            item["plan_description"] = description
    43→        if note:
    44→            item["plan_note"] = note
    45→        if override.get("cluster"):
    46→            cluster_name = override["cluster"]
    47→            cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
    48→            item["plan_cluster"] = {
    49→                "name": cluster_name,
    50→                "description": cluster_data.get("description"),
    51→                "total_items": len(cluster_data.get("issue_ids", [])),
    52→                "action_steps": cluster_data.get("action_steps") or [],
    53→            }
    54→
    55→
    56→def stamp_plan_sort_keys(
    57→    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
    58→    plan: dict,
    59→    new_ids: set[str],
    60→) -> None:
    61→    """Stamp ``_plan_position`` and ``_is_new`` on each item.
    62→
    63→    These fields are consumed by :func:`item_sort_key` in ``ranking.py``
    64→    to produce the correct final ordering in a single sort pass.
    65→    """
    66→    queue_order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    67→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    68→
    69→    position_map: dict[str, int] = {}
    70→    for idx, issue_id in enumerate(queue_order):
    71→        if issue_id not in skipped_ids:
    72→            position_map[issue_id] = idx
    73→
    74→    for item in items:
    75→        item_id = item["id"]
    76→        pos = position_map.get(item_id)
    77→        item["_plan_position"] = pos  # None if not in queue_order
    78→        item["_is_new"] = item_id in new_ids
    79→
    80→
    81→def separate_skipped(
    82→    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
    83→    plan: dict,
    84→) -> tuple[list[WorkQueueItem], list[WorkQueueItem]]:
    85→    """Separate skipped items from the main list.
    86→
    87→    Returns ``(non_skipped, skipped)`` so callers can optionally re-append.
    88→    """
    89→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    90→    if not skipped_ids:
    91→        return items, []
    92→    non_skipped: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    93→    skipped: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    94→    for item in items:
    95→        if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
    96→            skipped.append(item)
    97→        else:
    98→            non_skipped.append(item)
    99→    return non_skipped, skipped
   100→
   101→
   102→def filter_cluster_focus(
   103→    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
   104→    plan: dict,
   105→    cluster: str | None,
   106→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   107→    """Filter to only cluster members when a cluster focus is active."""
   108→    effective_cluster = cluster or plan.get("active_cluster")
   109→    if not effective_cluster:
   110→        return items
   111→    clusters: dict = plan.get("clusters", {})
   112→    cluster_data = clusters.get(effective_cluster, {})
   113→    cluster_member_ids = set(cluster_data.get("issue_ids", []))
   114→    if not cluster_member_ids:
   115→        return items
   116→    return [item for item in items if item["id"] in cluster_member_ids]
   117→
   118→
   119→def stamp_positions(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> None:
   120→    """Stamp queue_position and plan_skipped metadata on each item."""
   121→    skipped_map: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
   122→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(skipped_map.keys())
   123→
   124→    for position, item in enumerate(items):
   125→        item["queue_position"] = position + 1
   126→        if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
   127→            item["plan_skipped"] = True
   128→            skip_entry = skipped_map.get(item["id"])
   129→            if skip_entry:
   130→                item["plan_skip_kind"] = skip_entry.get("kind", "temporary")
   131→                skip_reason = skip_entry.get("reason")
   132→                if skip_reason:
   133→                    item["plan_skip_reason"] = skip_reason
   134→
   135→
   136→def action_type_for_detector(detector: str) -> str:
   137→    """Look up the action_type for a detector from the registry."""
   138→    meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   139→    if meta:
   140→        return meta.action_type
   141→    return "manual_fix"
   142→
   143→
   144→def _build_cluster_meta(
   145→    cluster_name: str, members: list[WorkQueueItem], cluster_data: dict[str, Any]
   146→) -> WorkQueueItem:
   147→    """Build a cluster meta-item from its member items."""
   148→    detector = members[0].get("detector", "") if members else ""
   149→    action = cluster_data.get("action") or ""
   150→    if "desloppify autofix" in action:
   151→        action_type = "auto_fix"
   152→    elif "desloppify move" in action:
   153→        action_type = "reorganize"
   154→    else:
   155→        action_type = action_type_for_detector(detector)
   156→        if action_type == "auto_fix" and "desloppify autofix" not in action:
   157→            action_type = "refactor"
   158→
   159→    stored_desc = cluster_data.get("description") or ""
   160→    total_in_cluster = len(cluster_data.get("issue_ids", []))
   161→    if stored_desc and total_in_cluster != len(members):
   162→        summary = stored_desc.replace(str(total_in_cluster), str(len(members)))
   163→    else:
   164→        summary = stored_desc or f"{len(members)} issues"
   165→
   166→    primary_command = cluster_data.get("action")
   167→    if not primary_command:
   168→        primary_command = f"desloppify next --cluster {cluster_name} --count 10"
   169→
   170→    estimated_impact = max(
   171→        (m.get("estimated_impact", 0.0) for m in members), default=0.0
   172→    )
   173→
   174→    return {
   175→        "id": cluster_name,
   176→        "kind": "cluster",
   177→        "action_type": action_type,
   178→        "summary": summary,
   179→        "members": members,
   180→        "member_count": len(members),
   181→        "primary_command": primary_command,
   182→        "cluster_name": cluster_name,
   183→        "cluster_auto": True,
   184→        "cluster_optional": bool(cluster_data.get("optional")),
   185→        "confidence": "high",
   186→        "detector": detector,
   187→        "file": "",
   188→        "estimated_impact": estimated_impact,
   189→        "action_steps": cluster_data.get("action_steps") or [],
   190→    }
   191→
   192→
   193→def collapse_clusters(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   194→    """Replace cluster member items with single cluster meta-items.
   195→
   196→    Walks the list in order: the first member of each collapsed cluster is
   197→    replaced with its meta-item, subsequent members are skipped.  This
   198→    preserves the ordering established by sort + plan-order.
   199→    """
   200→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   201→    if not clusters:
   202→        return items
   203→
   204→    fid_to_cluster: dict[str, str] = {}
   205→    for name, cluster in clusters.items():
   206→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
   207→            continue
   208→        for issue_id in cluster.get("issue_ids", []):
   209→            fid_to_cluster[issue_id] = name
   210→
   211→    if not fid_to_cluster:
   212→        return items
   213→
   214→    # Collect members per cluster (preserving encounter order)
   215→    cluster_members: dict[str, list[WorkQueueItem]] = {}
   216→    for item in items:
   217→        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
   218→        if cname:
   219→            cluster_members.setdefault(cname, []).append(item)
   220→
   221→    # Build meta-items only for clusters with 2+ members in the queue
   222→    meta_items: dict[str, WorkQueueItem] = {}
   223→    for cname, members in cluster_members.items():
   224→        if len(members) < 2:
   225→            continue
   226→        meta_items[cname] = _build_cluster_meta(
   227→            cname, members, clusters.get(cname, {})
   228→        )
   229→
   230→    # Walk in order: replace first member of each collapsed cluster
   231→    # with its meta-item, skip subsequent members
   232→    seen_clusters: set[str] = set()
   233→    result: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
   234→    for item in items:
   235→        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
   236→        if cname and cname in meta_items:
   237→            if cname not in seen_clusters:
   238→                seen_clusters.add(cname)
   239→                result.append(meta_items[cname])
   240→            # skip individual member
   241→        else:
   242→            result.append(item)
   243→    return result
   244→
   245→
   246→__all__ = [
   247→    "collapse_clusters",
   248→    "enrich_plan_metadata",
   249→    "filter_cluster_focus",
   250→    "new_item_ids",
   251→    "separate_skipped",
   252→    "stamp_plan_sort_keys",
   253→    "stamp_positions",
   254→]
   255→

1→"""Lifecycle and reset mutations for plan operations."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.promoted_ids import prune_promoted_ids
     6→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
     7→    PlanModel,
     8→    SkipEntry,
     9→    empty_plan,
    10→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    11→)
    12→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    13→
    14→
    15→def set_focus(plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str) -> None:
    16→    """Set the active cluster focus."""
    17→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    18→    if cluster_name not in plan["clusters"]:
    19→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
    20→    plan["active_cluster"] = cluster_name
    21→
    22→
    23→def clear_focus(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
    24→    """Clear the active cluster focus."""
    25→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    26→    plan["active_cluster"] = None
    27→
    28→
    29→def reset_plan(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
    30→    """Reset plan to empty state, preserving version and created timestamp.
    31→
    32→    Sets ``plan_start_scores`` to a sentinel so the next scan seeds real
    33→    scores instead of incorrectly treating the reset as a completed cycle.
    34→    """
    35→    created = plan.get("created", utc_now())
    36→    plan.clear()
    37→    for k, v in empty_plan().items():
    38→        plan[k] = v
    39→    plan["created"] = created
    40→    plan["plan_start_scores"] = {"reset": True}
    41→
    42→
    43→def purge_ids(plan: PlanModel, issue_ids: list[str]) -> int:
    44→    """Remove issue IDs from the plan entirely.
    45→
    46→    Cleans queue_order, skipped, and all cluster memberships.
    47→    Does NOT touch overrides (descriptions/notes are kept for history).
    48→    Returns count of IDs that were actually present somewhere.
    49→    """
    50→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    51→    found = 0
    52→
    53→    purge_set = set(issue_ids)
    54→    prune_promoted_ids(plan, purge_set)
    55→
    56→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
    57→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
    58→    for fid in issue_ids:
    59→        was_present = False
    60→        if fid in order:
    61→            order.remove(fid)
    62→            was_present = True
    63→        if fid in skipped:
    64→            skipped.pop(fid)
    65→            was_present = True
    66→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    67→            ids = cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
    68→            if fid in ids:
    69→                ids.remove(fid)
    70→                was_present = True
    71→        override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(fid)
    72→        if override and override.get("cluster"):
    73→            override["cluster"] = None
    74→            override["updated_at"] = utc_now()
    75→        if was_present:
    76→            found += 1
    77→
    78→    return found
    79→
    80→
    81→__all__ = ["clear_focus", "purge_ids", "reset_plan", "set_focus"]
    82→
```

1→"""Resolve command handlers."""

    10→from desloppify import state as state_mod

    12→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang

    31→

    33→from .queue_guard import _check_queue_order_guard
    34→from .render import (
    35→    _print_next_command,

    41→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.attestation import (
    42→    show_note_length_requirement,
    43→    validate_note_length,
    44→)
    45→
    46→from .selection import (
    47→    ResolveQueryContext,
    48→    _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation,
    49→    _previous_score_snapshot,
    50→    _validate_resolve_inputs,
    51→)
    52→
    53→_logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    54→
    55→
    56→class ClusterContext(NamedTuple):
    57→    cluster_name: str | None
    58→    cluster_completed: bool
    59→    cluster_remaining: int
    60→
    61→
    62→def _capture_cluster_context(
    63→    plan: dict, resolved_ids: list[str],
    64→) -> ClusterContext:
    65→    """Determine cluster membership for resolved issues, pre-purge."""
    66→    clusters = plan.get("clusters") or {}
    67→    overrides = plan.get("overrides") or {}
    68→    # Find the cluster the first resolved issue belongs to
    69→    cluster_name: str | None = None
    70→    for rid in resolved_ids:
    71→        ov = overrides.get(rid)
    72→        if ov and ov.get("cluster"):
    73→            cluster_name = ov["cluster"]
    74→            break
    75→    if not cluster_name or cluster_name not in clusters:
    76→        return ClusterContext(cluster_name=None, cluster_completed=False, cluster_remaining=0)
    77→    # Count how many will remain after these ids are removed
    78→    current_ids = set(clusters[cluster_name].get("issue_ids") or [])
    79→    remaining = current_ids - set(resolved_ids)
    80→    return ClusterContext(
    81→        cluster_name=cluster_name,
    82→        cluster_completed=len(remaining) == 0,
    83→        cluster_remaining=len(remaining),
    84→    )
    85→
    86→
    87→def _validate_fixed_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> bool:
    88→    if args.status != "fixed":
    89→        return True
    90→    note = getattr(args, "note", None)
    91→    if validate_note_length(note):
    92→        return True
    93→    show_note_length_requirement(note)
    94→    return False
    95→
    96→
    97→def _update_living_plan_after_resolve(
    98→    *,
    99→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   100→    all_resolved: list[str],
   101→    attestation: str | None,
   102→) -> tuple[dict | None, ClusterContext]:
   103→    plan = None
   104→    ctx = ClusterContext(cluster_name=None, cluster_completed=False, cluster_remaining=0)
   105→    try:
   106→        if not has_living_plan():
   107→            return None, ctx
   108→        plan = load_plan()
   109→        ctx = _capture_cluster_context(plan, all_resolved)
   110→        purged = purge_ids(plan, all_resolved)
   111→        append_log_entry(
   112→            plan,
   113→            "resolve",
   114→            issue_ids=all_resolved,
   115→            actor="user",
   116→            note=getattr(args, "note", None),

   141→def cmd_resolve(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   142→    """Resolve issue(s) matching one or more patterns."""
   143→    attestation = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   144→    _validate_resolve_inputs(args, attestation)

   148→    state_file = state_path(args)

   153→
   154→    if args.status == "fixed":

   157→            bypass=bool(getattr(args, "force_resolve", False)),

   164→        attestation=attestation,
   165→        resolve_all_patterns_fn=_resolve_all_patterns,
   166→    )
   167→    prev = _previous_score_snapshot(state)

   170→        for dim, payload in (state.get("subjective_assessments") or {}).items()
   171→        if isinstance(dim, str)

   173→
   174→    all_resolved = _resolve_all_patterns(state, args, attestation=attestation)
   175→    if not all_resolved:

   177→        print(colorize(f"No {status_label} issues matching: {' '.join(args.patterns)}", "yellow"))
   178→        return
   179→
   180→    save_state_or_exit(state, state_file)
   181→
   182→    plan, cluster_ctx = _update_living_plan_after_resolve(
   183→        args=args,
   184→        all_resolved=all_resolved,
   185→        attestation=attestation,
   186→    )
   187→    mid_cluster = (
   188→        cluster_ctx.cluster_name is not None and not cluster_ctx.cluster_completed
   189→    )
   190→
   191→    _print_resolve_summary(status=args.status, all_resolved=all_resolved)
   192→    _print_wontfix_batch_warning(
   193→        state,
   194→        status=args.status,
   195→        resolved_count=len(all_resolved),
   196→    )
   197→    show_score_with_plan_context(state, prev)
   198→    if not mid_cluster:
   199→        render_commit_guidance(state, plan, all_resolved, args.status)

)

_logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class ClusterContext(NamedTuple):
    cluster_name: str | None
    cluster_completed: bool
    cluster_remaining: int


def _capture_cluster_context(
    plan: dict, resolved_ids: list[str],
) -> ClusterContext:
    """Determine cluster membership for resolved issues, pre-purge."""
    clusters = plan.get("clusters") or {}
    overrides = plan.get("overrides") or {}
    # Find the cluster the first resolved issue belongs to
    cluster_name: str | None = None
    for rid in resolved_ids:
        ov = overrides.get(rid)
        if ov and ov.get("cluster"):
            cluster_name = ov["cluster"]
            break
    if not cluster_name or cluster_name not in clusters:
        return ClusterContext(cluster_name=None, cluster_completed=False, cluster_remaining=0)
    # Count how many will remain after these ids are removed
    current_ids = set(clusters[cluster_name].get("issue_ids") or [])
    remaining = current_ids - set(resolved_ids)
    return ClusterContext(
        cluster_name=cluster_name,
        cluster_completed=len(remaining) == 0,
        cluster_remaining=len(remaining),
    )


def _validate_fixed_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> bool:
    if args.status != "fixed":
        return True
    note = getattr(args, "note", None)
    if validate_note_length(note):
        return True
    show_note_length_requirement(note)
    return False


def _update_living_plan_after_resolve(
    *,
    args: argparse.Namespace,
    all_resolved: list[str],
    attestation: str | None,
) -> tuple[dict | None, ClusterContext]:
    plan = None
    ctx = ClusterContext(cluster_name=None, cluster_completed=False, cluster_remaining=0)
    try:
        if not has_living_plan():
            return None, ctx
        plan = load_plan()
        ctx = _capture_cluster_context(plan, all_resolved)
        purged = purge_ids(plan, all_resolved)
        append_log_entry(
            plan,
            "resolve",
            issue_ids=all_resolved,
            actor="user",
            note=getattr(args, "note", None),
            detail={"status": args.status, "attestation": attestation},
        )
        if ctx.cluster_completed and ctx.cluster_name:
            append_log_entry(
                plan,
                "cluster_done",
                issue_ids=all_resolved,
                cluster_name=ctx.cluster_name,
                actor="user",
            )
        # Commit tracking: add to uncommitted on fix, remove on reopen
        if args.status == "fixed":
            add_uncommitted_issues(plan, all_resolved)
        elif args.status == "open":

    for item in items:

        pos = position_map.get(item_id)

def separate_skipped(
    items: list[WorkQueueItem],
    plan: dict,
) -> tuple[list[WorkQueueItem], list[WorkQueueItem]]:
    """Separate skipped items from the main list.

    Returns ``(non_skipped, skipped)`` so callers can optionally re-append.
    """
    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    if not skipped_ids:
        return items, []
    non_skipped: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    skipped: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    for item in items:
        if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
            skipped.append(item)
        else:
            non_skipped.append(item)
    return non_skipped, skipped
```

    82→    """Typed shape of the dict returned by :func:`build_work_queue`."""
    83→
    84→    items: list[WorkQueueItem]
    85→    total: int
    86→    grouped: dict[str, list[WorkQueueItem]]
    87→    new_ids: set[str]
    88→
    89→
    90→def build_work_queue(
    91→    state: StateModel,
    92→    *,
    93→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
    94→) -> WorkQueueResult:
    95→    """Build a ranked work queue from state issues.
    96→
    97→    Pipeline:
    98→    1. Gather    — issue items, subjective dimensions, workflow stages
    99→    2. Score     — estimate impact from dimension headroom
   100→    3. Presort   — stamp plan positions, separate skipped items
   101→    4. Lifecycle — filter endgame-only items when objective work remains
   102→    5. Sort      — rank by impact/confidence, apply plan order
   103→    6. Limit     — truncate to count, optionally add explain metadata
   104→    """
   105→    opts = options or QueueBuildOptions()
   106→    plan, scan_path, status, threshold = _resolve_inputs(opts, state)
   107→
   108→    # 1. Gather
   109→    items = build_issue_items(
   110→        state, scan_path=scan_path, status_filter=status,
   111→        scope=opts.scope, chronic=opts.chronic,
   112→    )
   113→    items += _gather_subjective_items(state, opts, threshold)
   114→    items += _gather_workflow_items(state, plan, status)
   115→
   116→    # 2. Score
   117→    enrich_with_impact(items, state.get("dimension_scores", {}))
   118→
   119→    # 3. Plan-aware ordering (part 1: separate skipped items)
   120→    new_ids, skipped = _plan_presort(items, state, plan)
   121→
   122→    # 4. Lifecycle filter — endgame-only items filtered when objective work remains
   123→    items = _apply_lifecycle_filter(items)
   124→
   125→    # 5. Sort & plan post-processing
   126→    items.sort(key=item_sort_key)
   127→    _plan_postsort(items, skipped, plan, opts)
   128→
   129→    # 6. Finalize
   130→    if not items:
   131→        items += _empty_queue_fallback(plan)
   132→    total = len(items)
   133→    if opts.count is not None and opts.count > 0:
   134→        items = items[:opts.count]
   135→    if opts.explain:
   136→        for item in items:
   137→            item["explain"] = item_explain(item)
   138→
   139→    return {
   140→        "items": items,
   141→        "total": total,
   142→        "grouped": group_queue_items(items, "item"),
   143→        "new_ids": new_ids,
   144→    }
   145→
   146→
   147→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   148→# Pipeline helpers (private to this module)
   149→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   150→
   151→
   152→def _resolve_inputs(
   153→    opts: QueueBuildOptions, state: StateModel,
   154→) -> tuple[dict | None, str | None, str, float]:
   155→    """Resolve plan, scan_path, status, and subjective threshold from options."""
   156→    ctx = opts.context
   157→    plan = ctx.plan if ctx is not None else opts.plan
   158→
   159→    scan_path: str | None = (
   160→        state.get("scan_path")
   161→        if opts.scan_path is _SCAN_PATH_FROM_STATE
   162→        else opts.scan_path  # type: ignore[assignment]
   163→    )
   164→
   165→    status = opts.status
   166→    if status not in ALL_STATUSES:
   167→        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported status filter: {status}")
   168→
   169→    try:
   170→        threshold = float(opts.subjective_threshold)
   171→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   172→        threshold = 100.0
   173→    threshold = max(0.0, min(100.0, threshold))
   174→
   175→    return plan, scan_path, status, threshold
   176→
   177→
   178→def _gather_subjective_items(
   179→    state: StateModel,
   180→    opts: QueueBuildOptions,
   181→    threshold: float,
   182→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   183→    """Build subjective dimension candidates.
   184→
   185→    Lifecycle filtering (endgame gating) happens in _apply_lifecycle_filter,
   186→    not here. This function only handles configuration and scope.
   187→    """
   188→    if not opts.include_subjective:
   189→        return []
   190→    if opts.status not in {"open", "all"}:
   191→        return []
   192→    if opts.chronic:
   193→        return []
   194→
   195→    candidates = build_subjective_items(
   196→        state, state.get("issues", {}), threshold=threshold,
   197→    )
   198→    return [item for item in candidates if scope_matches(item, opts.scope)]
   199→
   200→
   201→def _gather_workflow_items(
   202→    state: StateModel, plan: dict | None, status: str,
   203→) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   204→    """Inject triage stages, checkpoints, and create-plan when plan is active."""
   205→    if not plan or status not in {"open", "all"}:
   206→        return []
   207→
   208→    items: list[WorkQueueItem] = list(build_triage_stage_items(plan, state))
   209→    for builder in (
   210→        build_score_checkpoint_item,
   211→        build_import_scores_item,
   212→        build_communicate_score_item,
   213→    ):
   214→        item = builder(plan, state)
   215→        if item is not None:
   216→            items.append(item)
   217→    plan_item = build_create_plan_item(plan)
   218→    if plan_item is not None:
   219→        items.append(plan_item)
   220→    return items
   221→
   222→
   223→
   224→def _plan_presort(
   225→    items: list[WorkQueueItem], state: StateModel, plan: dict | None,
   226→) -> tuple[set[str], list[WorkQueueItem]]:
   227→    """Enrich plan metadata and stamp sort keys before sorting.
   228→
   229→    Returns ``(new_ids, skipped)`` — skipped items are removed from
   230→    ``items`` in place and returned separately for post-sort re-append.
   231→    """
   232→    if not plan:
   233→        return set(), []
   234→
   235→    new_ids = _new_item_ids(state)
   236→    enrich_plan_metadata(items, plan)
   237→    stamp_plan_sort_keys(items, plan, new_ids)
   238→    remaining, skipped = separate_skipped(items, plan)
   239→    items[:] = remaining
   240→    return new_ids, skipped
   241→
   242→

   245→    skipped: list[WorkQueueItem],

   247→    opts: QueueBuildOptions,
   248→) -> None:

   260→def _has_objective_items(items: list[WorkQueueItem]) -> bool:
   261→    """True if any objective mechanical work items remain in the queue."""
   262→    return any(
   263→        i.get("kind") == "issue"
   264→        and i.get("detector", "") not in NON_OBJECTIVE_DETECTORS
   265→        for i in items
   266→    )
   267→
   268→
   269→def _has_initial_reviews(items: list[WorkQueueItem]) -> bool:
   270→    """True if any unassessed subjective dimensions need initial review."""
   271→    return any(
   272→        i.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension"
   273→        and i.get("initial_review")
   274→        for i in items
   275→    )
   276→
   277→
   278→def _is_endgame_only(item: WorkQueueItem) -> bool:
   279→    """True if this item should only appear when the objective queue is drained."""
   280→    return (
   281→        item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension"
   282→        and not item.get("initial_review")
   283→    )
   284→
   285→
   286→def _apply_lifecycle_filter(items: list[WorkQueueItem]) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   287→    """Enforce lifecycle visibility rules.
   288→
   289→    The queue enforces a phase order: scan → initial review → objective work.
   290→
   291→    1. **Initial reviews pending** → only show initial-review subjective items.
   292→       The user must complete first-time reviews before working objective items.
   293→    2. **Objective work remains** → show objective items, hide endgame-only
   294→       subjective reassessments (stale re-reviews).
   295→    3. **Objective drained** → everything visible (endgame).
   296→    """
   297→    if _has_initial_reviews(items):
   298→        # Phase 1: only initial reviews visible — triage and workflow
   299→        # items stay hidden until initial reviews are complete.
   300→        return [
   301→            i for i in items
   302→            if i.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension" and i.get("initial_review")
   303→        ]
   304→    if not _has_objective_items(items):
   305→        return items  # endgame: everything visible
   306→    return [i for i in items if not _is_endgame_only(i)]

193→def collapse_clusters(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> list[WorkQueueItem]:
   194→    """Replace cluster member items with single cluster meta-items.
   195→
   196→    Walks the list in order: the first member of each collapsed cluster is
   197→    replaced with its meta-item, subsequent members are skipped.  This
   198→    preserves the ordering established by sort + plan-order.
   199→    """
   200→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   201→    if not clusters:
   202→        return items
   203→
   204→    fid_to_cluster: dict[str, str] = {}
   205→    for name, cluster in clusters.items():
   206→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
   207→            continue
   208→        for issue_id in cluster.get("issue_ids", []):
   209→            fid_to_cluster[issue_id] = name
   210→
   211→    if not fid_to_cluster:
   212→        return items
   213→
   214→    # Collect members per cluster (preserving encounter order)
   215→    cluster_members: dict[str, list[WorkQueueItem]] = {}
   216→    for item in items:
   217→        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
   218→        if cname:
   219→            cluster_members.setdefault(cname, []).append(item)
   220→
   221→    # Build meta-items only for clusters with 2+ members in the queue
   222→    meta_items: dict[str, WorkQueueItem] = {}
   223→    for cname, members in cluster_members.items():
   224→        if len(members) < 2:
   225→            continue
   226→        meta_items[cname] = _build_cluster_meta(
   227→            cname, members, clusters.get(cname, {})
   228→        )
   229→
   230→    # Walk in order: replace first member of each collapsed cluster
   231→    # with its meta-item, skip subsequent members
   232→    seen_clusters: set[str] = set()
   233→    result: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
   234→    for item in items:
   235→        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
   236→        if cname and cname in meta_items:
   237→            if cname not in seen_clusters:
   238→                seen_clusters.add(cname)
   239→                result.append(meta_items[cname])
   240→            # skip individual member
   241→        else:
   242→            result.append(item)
   243→    return result
   244→
   245→
   246→__all__ = [
   247→    "collapse_clusters",
   248→    "enrich_plan_metadata",
   249→    "filter_cluster_focus",
   250→    "new_item_ids",
   251→    "separate_skipped",
   252→    "stamp_plan_sort_keys",
   253→    "stamp_positions",
   254→]
   255→

Perfect! Now I have enough information to provide a comprehensive answer. Let me create the final report:

## Summary: Cluster-Issue Resolution Interaction in Desloppify

Based on a thorough analysis of the desloppify codebase, here's how clusters interact with issue resolution:

### 1. How Reconciliation Handles Cluster `issue_ids` When Issues Get `auto_resolved`

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile.py`

The reconciliation system uses the concept of **"alive" issues** (lines 51-56):
```python
def _is_issue_alive(state: StateModel, issue_id: str) -> bool:
    """Return True if the issue exists and is open."""
    issue = state.get("issues", {}).get(issue_id)
    if issue is None:
        return False
    return issue.get("status") == "open"
```

Only issues with `status == "open"` are considered alive. When an issue gets `auto_resolved` (or any other non-open status like `fixed`, `wontfix`, `false_positive`), it is no longer "alive."

**The `_supersede_id` Function** (lines 59-115) handles disappeared/resolved issues:
- Moves the issue to the `plan["superseded"]` dict
- **Crucially removes the issue from all cluster `issue_ids` lists** (lines 104-107):
  ```python
  for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
      ids = cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
      if issue_id in ids:
          ids.remove(issue_id)
  ```
- Also removes it from `queue_order`, `skipped`, `promoted_ids`, and clears cluster references in overrides

**Epic Cluster Reconciliation** (lines 181-196):
- For epic clusters (those starting with `"epic/"`), alive issues are filtered
- **Empty epic clusters are automatically deleted** when they have no alive issues:
  ```python
  alive_ids = [fid for fid in issue_ids if _is_issue_alive(state, fid)]
  if alive_ids != issue_ids:
      cluster["issue_ids"] = alive_ids
      result.changes += 1
  if not alive_ids:
      epic_names_to_delete.append(name)
  ```

### 2. Is There a Concept of a Cluster Being "Done" or "Completed"?

**Yes, but it's event-based, not state-based.**

**`ClusterContext` in resolve command** (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py`, lines 56-84):
```python
class ClusterContext(NamedTuple):
    cluster_name: str | None
    cluster_completed: bool
    cluster_remaining: int

def _capture_cluster_context(plan: dict, resolved_ids: list[str]) -> ClusterContext:
    """Determine cluster membership for resolved issues, pre-purge."""
    # ... calculates remaining issues AFTER purging resolved ones
    remaining = current_ids - set(resolved_ids)
    return ClusterContext(
        cluster_name=cluster_name,
        cluster_completed=len(remaining) == 0,  # True when all issues removed
        cluster_remaining=len(remaining),
    )
```

**The `cluster_done` Event** (lines 119-126):
When a user resolves issue(s) and their cluster becomes empty, a `"cluster_done"` action is logged:
```python
if ctx.cluster_completed and ctx.cluster_name:
    append_log_entry(
        plan,
        "cluster_done",
        issue_ids=all_resolved,
        cluster_name=ctx.cluster_name,
        actor="user",
    )
```

**Key Point:** There is **no persistent `status` field tracking "done"** — completion is detected dynamically by comparing `cluster["issue_ids"]` length before/after a resolve operation. The schema defines a `status: str` field on clusters (line 70 of schema.py), but this is used for epic clusters to track triage status like `"pending"` or `"open"`, not to track work completion.

### 3. How `separate_skipped` in `plan_order.py` Works

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/plan_order.py`, lines 81-99

```python
def separate_skipped(items: list[WorkQueueItem], plan: dict) -> tuple[list[WorkQueueItem], list[WorkQueueItem]]:
    """Separate skipped items from the main list.
    Returns ``(non_skipped, skipped)`` so callers can optionally re-append.
    """
    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    if not skipped_ids:
        return items, []
    non_skipped: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    skipped: list[WorkQueueItem] = []
    for item in items:
        if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
            skipped.append(item)
        else:
            non_skipped.append(item)
    return non_skipped, skipped
```

**Does it look at issue status?** **No** — it only checks the plan's `plan["skipped"]` dictionary. The `skipped` dict holds `SkipEntry` objects with metadata like `kind` ("temporary", "permanent", "false_positive"), `reason`, and `review_after`. 

**Important distinction:**
- **Skipped** (in `plan["skipped"]`): Issues that the user has explicitly deferred (wontfix, false positive, temporary skip)
- **Resolved** (status != "open"): Issues that disappeared from state or were marked as fixed/auto_resolved

Skipped items can still exist in state as open issues (though they shouldn't be). Resolution (when status changes) is handled by reconciliation, not by `separate_skipped`.

### 4. What Happens to a Cluster's Queue Position When All Its Issues Are Resolved

**Automatic Removal via `purge_ids`:**

When issues are resolved, `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py` (line 110) calls:
```python
purged = purge_ids(plan, all_resolved)
```

This function (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/operations_lifecycle.py`, lines 43-78) removes the issue from:
- `queue_order` list
- `skipped` dict
- All cluster `issue_ids` lists
- Clears cluster references in overrides

**What happens to the cluster itself:**
- **Auto-clusters** are pruned by `prune_stale_clusters()` (auto_cluster_sync.py, lines 390-418):
  - If the cluster becomes empty (`issue_ids = []`) and is not in the active auto-key set, it's deleted
  - The cluster stays in `plan["clusters"]` if it has the same key as currently-detected clusters
  - If user-modified, the cluster keeps any remaining "alive" issues; empty user-modified clusters stay for now

- **Epic clusters** are removed during reconciliation if they have no alive issues (as shown above)

**Queue position:** The cluster's position in `queue_order` is determined by the **first** issue's position. When all issues are resolved:
- The cluster itself is removed from `clusters` dict
- `queue_order` contains no references to cluster names anyway (only issue IDs)
- If cluster collapse was in effect, the cluster meta-item simply never appears in the queue

**Cluster Focus:** If that cluster was the active focus (`plan["active_cluster"]`), the focus is cleared (lines 113-114 of operations_cluster.py and lines 415-416 of auto_cluster_sync.py).

---

## Key Invariants

1. **Alive = Open Only:** Only issues with `status == "open"` are considered active in clusters
2. **Reconciliation is Ruthless:** Any non-open issue is immediately removed from cluster membership
3. **No Persistent "Done" State:** Cluster completion is a transient event, not a stored state
4. **Separate Systems:** Skipping (user deferral) and resolution (status change) are orthogonal; reconciliation doesn't check skip status
5. **Cascade Cleanup:** When issues vanish, they're removed from clusters → clusters may become empty → empty clusters may be pruned

    64→def _resolve_state_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    65→    return path if path is not None else state_mod.STATE_FILE
    66→
    67→
    68→def _resolve_plan_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    69→    return path if path is not None else PLAN_FILE
    70→
    71→
    72→def _plan_file_for_state(state_file: Path | None) -> Path | None:
    73→    if state_file is None:
    74→        return None
    75→    return plan_path_for_state(state_file)
    76→
    77→
    78→def _snapshot_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    79→    if not path.exists():
    80→        return None
    81→    return path.read_text()
    82→
    83→
    84→def _restore_file_snapshot(path: Path, snapshot: str | None) -> None:
    85→    if snapshot is None:
    86→        try:
    87→            path.unlink()
    88→        except FileNotFoundError:
    89→            return
    90→        return
    91→    safe_write_text(path, snapshot)
    92→
    93→
    94→def _save_plan_state_transactional(
    95→    *,
    96→    plan: dict,
    97→    plan_path: Path | None,
    98→    state_data: dict,
    99→    state_path_value: Path | None,
   100→) -> None:
   101→    """Persist plan+state together; rollback both files on partial write failure."""
   102→    effective_plan_path = _resolve_plan_file(plan_path)
   103→    effective_state_path = _resolve_state_file(state_path_value)
   104→    plan_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_plan_path)
   105→    state_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_state_path)
   106→
   107→    try:
   108→        state_mod.save_state(state_data, effective_state_path)
   109→        save_plan(plan, effective_plan_path)
   110→    except Exception:
   111→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_state_path, state_snapshot)
   112→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_plan_path, plan_snapshot)
   113→        raise
   114→

   116→def cmd_plan_describe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   117→    """Set augmented description on issues."""
   118→    state = command_runtime(args).state
   119→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   120→        return
   121→
   122→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   123→    text: str = getattr(args, "text", "")
   124→
   125→    plan = load_plan()
   126→    issue_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   127→    if not issue_ids:
   128→        print(colorize("  No matching issues found.", "yellow"))
   129→        return
   130→
   131→    for fid in issue_ids:
   132→        describe_issue(plan, fid, text or None)
   133→    append_log_entry(
   134→        plan, "describe", issue_ids=issue_ids, actor="user",
   135→        detail={"text": text or None},
   136→    )
   137→    save_plan(plan)
   138→    print(colorize(f"  Set description on {len(issue_ids)} issue(s).", "green"))
   139→

   171→def _validate_skip_requirements(
   172→    *,
   173→    kind: str,
   174→    attestation: str | None,
   175→    note: str | None,
   176→) -> bool:
   177→    if not skip_kind_requires_attestation(kind):
   178→        return True
   179→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   180→        show_attestation_requirement(
   181→            "Permanent skip" if kind == "permanent" else "False positive",
   182→            attestation,
   183→            ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
   184→        )
   185→        return False
   186→    if skip_kind_requires_note(kind) and not note:
   187→        print(
   188→            colorize("  --permanent requires --note to explain the decision.", "yellow"),
   189→            file=sys.stderr,
   190→        )
   191→        return False
   192→    return True

   195→def _apply_state_skip_resolution(
   196→    *,
   197→    kind: str,
   198→    state_file: Path | None,
   199→    issue_ids: list[str],
   200→    note: str | None,
   201→    attestation: str | None,
   202→) -> dict | None:
   203→    status = skip_kind_state_status(kind)
   204→    if status is None:
   205→        return None
   206→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   207→    for fid in issue_ids:
   208→        state_mod.resolve_issues(
   209→            state_data,
   210→            fid,
   211→            status,
   212→            note or "",
   213→            attestation=attestation,
   214→        )
   215→    return state_data
